# Copy corrections

Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

Companion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This
says what has actually gone wrong and why.

Started 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a
correction is made.

## The rules these corrections came from

Three rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was
enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface
added later starts uncovered.

- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.
- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`
  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.
- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.

Guards now live with the content rather than with each component:
`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every
surface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.

## Corrections

"A Personalized If/Then checks the translation. Read the ordinary meaning, imagine the proposed sufficient situation, and ask what the sentence makes you know. Then try the reverse and compare it with the original claim. The What Test helps identify the complete idea an expression refers to. Both checks serve meaning, not a memorized word-position rule.
"Ask what the expression refers to, then read your arrow in English. In “A report is reviewed if it is submitted before noon,” review comes first, but submission before noon is what guarantees it. Keep the full before-noon submission condition on the sufficient side.
"Find the complete sufficient idea in each Group 1 expression and preserve its meaning in a diagram.
"Group 2 helps us find the necessary side: what the sentence says is required. Its core expressions are only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires and must in their relevant uses. We
"Has a morning-tour booking belongs right because it is required for receiving a map. Receives map → Has morning-tour booking. Read only if as one expression. It contains if, but it does not make the booking enough for a map.
"Keep the legally-binding restriction; classify each expression separately.
"Keep the matching transaction record and classify expressions separately.
"Knowing a vehicle is permitted inside is therefore enough to establish that it is electric. The expression

"Read the expression in its sentence

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\grill-me

# Grill Me

One skill, self-contained. It holds both the **interview method** (Step 2) and the **capture discipline** (Steps 1 and 3): every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up. It does not call the vendor `grilling` skill: the method is written inline below so this skill can add the capture steps around it and keep working even if `grilling` changes upstream.

If the topic is a feature, component, or other implementation work rather than an open-ended plan or decision, `brainstorming` runs a similar interview but gates on a written, approved design doc before any code — reach for that instead when the destination is implementation.

## Step 1: set up the capture file (BEFORE the first question)

1. Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`; in an established project follow its declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.
2. Create the capture file at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create `brainstorms/` if missing). Every capture lives here, one predictable home regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. A polished deliverable (plan, map, spec) may later move into the relevant `projects/` folder; the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
   - Get today's real date from the clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
   - Never overwrite an existing capture. Same date and topic: add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user asks or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.
   - No topic supplied or inferable: create an `untitled-discovery` capture with goal pending, ask what to explore, record the chosen topic and goal in that same file.
3. Write the header immediately: title, date, goal of the session, empty "Open flags" section.
4. Tell the user where you are saving, in one line.

## Step 2: run the interview

Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.

Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you have not heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.

Format a round like so:

```
**Q1 - <question title>**: <question body, may be multiple paragraphs, including choices>

Recommended: <your recommended answer>

---

**Q2 - <question title>**: ...

Recommended: ...
```

Each round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.

Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, live site), look it up or dispatch a sub-agent; never ask the user for anything you could find yourself. Do not block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.

The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch visited, nothing silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

Two adjustments the capture layer imposes on the method:

- For **decisions**, give a recommended answer grounded in context and label it as a recommendation. For **personal or business facts**, ask neutrally. Never lead with invented facts, and never save your inference as the user's answer.
- When the user **cannot answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Do not stall the round on it.

## Step 3: the checkpoint rule (non-negotiable)

After EVERY round of answers, BEFORE you ask the next round:

- Append one structured entry per answered question: the question, the key facts and decisions from the answer (in the user's words where wording matters), and any flags (things they could not answer, plus who should).
- Update the running summary when a later answer changes it. Preserve the original entry, mark it superseded, and link to the correcting answer so the reasoning stays traceable.
- Read back the saved entries before continuing. If the write fails, say so, keep the answers visible in chat, and do not claim they were saved or collect more answers without a working checkpoint.
- Only then ask the next round.

Never let two rounds pile up in your head before writing. The file, not your context, is the source of truth. Never make the user ask you to save progress.

Keep user-confirmed facts, tentative ideas, your recommendations, and unresolved questions distinguishable in the capture.

## Capture file structure

```
# {Topic}: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes
Date: {date} · Goal: {one line}
Status: in progress / paused / complete
Context sources: {relevant existing pages, if any}

## Summary / key decisions
(running synthesis, updated as you go)

## Q&A log
### Round 1
#### Q1 - {topic}
- Asked: {question}
- Recommended: {your recommendation, labeled}
- Captured: {user-confirmed facts and decisions, in their words where it matters}
- Tentative: {unconfirmed ideas, clearly labeled}
- Flags: {open item -> owner}
#### Q2 - ...
```

## At the end or on pause

Respect an explicit stop immediately; do not add a new question. Near the end, offer a completeness backstop ("anything we haven't touched?"). Then:

- Read the capture for contradictions or gaps. Mark unresolved conflicts explicitly; do not choose a business fact on the user's behalf. Update status and resume point.
- For sessions explicitly about building or updating AI OS context, merge user-confirmed durable facts and preferences into the appropriate existing context page, with a dated link back to the capture. Preserve unrelated content. Record confirmed meaningful decisions in the existing decision log without duplicating entries. Unknowns, brainstorm ideas, and your recommendations stay labeled in the capture until confirmed.
- For a plan or design interview without a request to update canonical context, the raw capture is the output. Suggest `/link` for routing a useful result, or `/level-up` for turning a selected opportunity into one improvement. Do not run either automatically.
- Ensure the operating manual or an existing index routes to `brainstorms/` when capture routing is absent, using the project's conventions and preserving shared manual parity. Individual captures do not each need a root-manual entry. Raw captures are dated interview evidence, not automatically current business truth.
- Give a short recap: clickable capture path, context pages actually updated, remaining flags, next step or resume point.

Do not act on the plan until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

## Boundaries and verification

- Scope is the local interview capture and any requested context updates. No global-memory edits, publication, messages, or external-system changes. Do not request or save credentials; use references or redactions for secrets.
- The starter kit ignores `brainstorms/` in Git by default. This does not remove captures already tracked in an existing repository.
- Validate a fresh session, a same-day name collision, a resume, a correcting answer, a stop request, and a failed write. Verify that only confirmed facts reach canonical context and that earlier answers remain traceable.

One check first. The "That keeps the meaning." note in your screenshot only shows when the answer is right, so it isn't a bug. The wording is still odd, though, and Q4 asks about it.

**Round 1**

**Q1: What replaces "expression"?** The lessons use it in a few different ways:
- (a) the word being taught: "Recall the expressions", "seven expressions", "Group 1 expressions"
- (b) a multi-word indicator read as one unit: "Read only if as one expression", "The only: read the whole expression"
- (c) the condition it points to: "Identify the whole expression being denied", "Use the What Test on the full expression"

Recommended:
- (a) → "indicators"
- (b) → "phrase", as in "Read only if as one phrase"
- (c) → "idea" or "condition", as in "the whole condition being denied"

---

**Q2: "Establish" (311 uses) and "guarantee" (218).** Examples: "enough to establish the other condition" and "do not turn the requirement into a guarantee." Do these sound like you, or too formal?

Recommended: keep "guarantee", since it's plain and memorable. Swap most uses of "establish" for "prove" or "tells you", as in "enough to prove the other condition."

---

**Q3: "Preserve" (116) and "relationship" (111).** Examples: "Which direction preserves the original rule?" and "recognize a conditional relationship."

Recommended:
- "preserve" → "keeps", as in "Which direction keeps the original rule?"
- "relationship" → "rule" where a rule is meant

---

**Q4: Feedback headings in the staged drills.** Right now a correct answer gets "That keeps the meaning." A wrong one gets "Revise this step." A written answer gets "Compare the meaning." On an indicator step, "That keeps the meaning" doesn't fit.

Recommended: give each step its own heading, like "Right indicator." or "Right direction." For a wrong answer, use "Not quite. Try again." What would you actually say out loud in a session?

---

**Q5: Other phrases. Mark each one keep, cut, or reword:**
- "Personalized If/Then" (13), the name of a check
- "the What Test" (the "If what? All what?" check)
- "complete idea" (49)
- "settled" / "not settled" (16), as in "Getting a bicycle is not settled."
- "stipulated rule", "classify"

Recommended:
- Keep "the What Test", "complete idea" and "not settled". They're short and they teach.
- Swap "stipulated" for "this rule".
- Swap "classify" for "sort".
- Personalized If/Then: your call.

ids, keys, classNames\n    for rx, rep in compiled:\n        def sub(m, rep=rep, rx=rx):\n            counts[rx.pattern] += 1\n            out = m.expand(rep)\n            return cap(m.group(0), out)\n        s = rx.sub(sub, s)\n    return s\n\nSTR = re.compile(r\"\"\"(?P<q>['\"`])(?P<body>(?:\\\\.|(?!(?P=q)).)*?)(?P=q)|>(?P<jsx>[^<>{}]+)<\"\"\", re.S)\n\ndef fix_line_text(text):\n    def sub(m):\n        if m.group('jsx') is not None:\n            return '>' + fix_prose(m.group('jsx')) + '<'\n        q, body = m.group('q'), m.group('body')\n        if q != '`' and '\\n' in body:\n            return m.group(0)\n        return q + fix_prose(body) + q\n    return STR.sub(sub, text)\n\nchanged = []\nfor f in sorted(root.rglob('*')):\n    if f.suffix not in ('.ts', '.tsx') or 'data' in f.parts:\n        continue\n    src = f.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\n    lines = src.split('\\n')\n    out = []\n    for ln in lines:\n        if 'originalStatement' in ln or ln.lstrip().startswith('import ') or '\"sha256\"' in ln:\n            out.append(ln)\n        else:\n            out.append(fix_line_text(ln))\n    new = '\\n'.join(out)\n    if new != src:\n        changed.append(f)\n        if not dry:\n            f.write_text(new, encoding='utf-8', newline='')\nprint(len(changed), 'files')\nfor k, v in counts.most_common():\n    print(v, k)\n"}

describe('the opening curriculum teaching contract', () => {
  it('introduces the rule before its two indicator groups', () => {
    expect(conditionalUnits.slice(0, 4).map(unit => unit.id)).toEqual(openingIds);
    expect(opening.map(unit => unit.prerequisites)).toEqual([
      [], ['orientation'], ['sufficient-necessary'], ['group-1'],
    ]);
  });

  it('keeps new explanations and practice in the shipped reference bundle', () => {
    for (const unit of opening) {
      expect(unit).toEqual(foundationUnits.find(source => source.id === unit.id));
      expect(JSON.stringify(unit)).not.toMatch(/\b(?:dogs?|mammals?)\b/i);
      if (unit.id !== 'orientation') {
        expect(unit.method.join(' ')).not.toMatch(/contrapositive|contraposition|chain|quantity/i);
        expect(unit.teaching.join(' ')).not.toMatch(/\b(?:contrapositive|contraposition|chains?|quantifiers?|quantities)\b/i);
      } else {
        // The approved reference-ordered opening develops contraposition before
        // notation. Independently assigned group notes keep their narrow scope.
        expect(unit.teaching.findIndex(text => /This is the contrapositive/.test(text))).toBeLessThan(
          unit.teaching.findIndex(text => /Write the relationship/.test(text)),
        );
        expect(unit.method).toHaveLength(5);
        expect(unit.method[3]).toMatch(/contrapositive/);
        expect(unit.method[4]).toMatch(/English/);
        expect(unit.teaching.join(' ')).not.toMatch(/\bunless\b|quantifiers?|quantities/i);
      }
    }
  });

  it('keeps the 62-question answer contract used by saved choices and diagrams', () => {
    // The pre-voice baseline preserves 61 current answer contracts after the
    // original three -v2 mappings. Actual inspection also replaces group-2-a1
    // with direct translation under its own -v2 ID, retaining the HEAD original.
    // Earlier group-2-b3 Every -> A wording preserves its universal always claim;
    // that cosmetic change and group-2-a2's obligation wording keep their keys.
    // orientation-m1 now uses the taught "usually" rather than "most". Normalize
    // that one deliberate wording edit for this baseline comparison, and check
    // its current wording explicitly below. Its ID, options and key stay intact.
    const contract = openingIds.flatMap(unitId => {
      const unit = opening.find(item => item.id === unitId)!;
      return levels.flatMap(level => unit.drills[level].map(question => [
        unit.id, level, question.id,
        question.id === 'orientation-m1'
          ? 'Most inspected lifts operate quietly. Lift 2 was inspected. Is quiet operation guaranteed?'
          : question.prompt,
        question.options, question.answerIndex,
      ]));
    });
    expect(contract).toHaveLength(62);

export function StagedReasoningDrill({ mode }: { mode: 'sufficient' | 'contrapositive' | 'mixed' }) {
  if (mode === 'mixed') return <div className="sr-mixed"><OpeningMixedDrill/></div>;
  return <StagedRun key={mode} storageKey={`conditional-staged-v1:${mode}`} items={mode === 'sufficient' ? sufficientStagedItems : contrapositiveStagedItems}/>;
}

function restore(storageKey: string, items: Item[]) {
  const fallback = { index: 0, step: 0, answers: {} as Record<string, Answer> };
  try {
    const data = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(storageKey) ?? 'null');
    if (!data || !data.answers || typeof data.answers !== 'object') return fallback;
    const index = Number.isInteger(data.index) && data.index >= 0 && data.index < items.length ? data.index : 0;
    const step = Number.isInteger(data.step) && data.step >= 0 && data.step < items[index].stages.length ? data.step : 0;
    const answers: Record<string, Answer> = {};
    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(data.answers)) {
      const a = value as Answer;
      const [i, s] = key.split(':').map(Number);
      if (!items[i]?.stages[s] || !a || typeof a.writing !== 'string' || !(a.choice === null || Number.isInteger(a.choice)) || typeof a.checked !== 'boolean' || typeof a.accepted !== 'boolean') continue;
      const stage = items[i].stages[s];
      if (a.choice !== null && (!stage.options || a.choice < 0 || a.choice >= stage.options.length)) continue;
      const checked = data.fingerprint === JSON.stringify(items) && a.checked;
      answers[key] = { ...a, checked, accepted: checked && a.accepted && (stage.options ? a.choice === stage.correct : !!a.writing.trim()) };
    }
    const firstUnfinished = items[index].stages.findIndex((_, s) => !answers[`${index}:${s}`]?.accepted);
    return { index, step: firstUnfinished < 0 ? step : Math.min(step, firstUnfinished), answers };
  } catch { return fallback; }
}

function StagedRun({ items, storageKey }: { items: Item[]; storageKey: string }) {
  const [initial] = useState(() => restore(storageKey, items));
  const [index, setIndex] = useState(initial.index);
  const [step, setStep] = useState(initial.step);
  const [answers, setAnswers] = useState<Record<string, Answer>>(initial.answers);
  const [storageFailed, setStorageFailed] = useState(false);
  const [finished, setFinished] = useState(false);
  const heading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);
  const feedback = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);
  const item = items[index], stage = item.stages[step], key = `${index}:${step}`;
  const answer = answers[key] ?? empty();
  const objective = !!stage.options;
  const correct = answer.choice === stage.correct;
  const completed = items.filter((q, i) => q.stages.every((_, s) => answers[`${i}:${s}`]?.accepted)).length;
  function update(patch: Partial<Answer>) { setAnswers(old => {
    const next = { ...old, [key]: { ...(old[key] ?? empty()), ...patch } };
    if (patch.accepted === false) for (let s = step + 1; s < item.stages.length; s++) {
      const later = `${index}:${s}`;
      if (next[later]) next[later] = { ...next[later], checked: false, accepted: false };
    }
    return next;
  }); }
  useEffect(() => { try { localStorage.setItem(storageKey, JSON.stringify({ index, step, answers, fingerprint: JSON.stringify(items) })); setStorageFailed(false); } catch { setStorageFailed(true); } }, [index, step, answers, items, storageKey]);
  useEffect(() => { heading.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true }); }, [step, index, finished]);
  useEffect(() => { if (answer.checked) feedback.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true }); }, [answer.checked]);
  function move(i: number) { setIndex(i); setStep(0); setFinished(false); }
  function next() { if (step < item.stages.length - 1) setStep(step + 1); else if (index < items.length - 1) move(index + 1); else setFinished(true); }
  return <section className="sr-run" aria-label="Step-by-step reasoning practice">
    {storageFailed && <p role="status" className="sr-storage-error">Your browser could not save this practice. Keep this page open to retain your work.</p>}
    <header className="sr-header"><span>YOUR PROGRESS</span><strong>{Math.round(completed / items.length * 100)}<small>%</small></strong><div role="progressbar" aria-label="Sentences completed" aria-valuenow={completed} aria-valuemin={0} aria-valuemax={items.length}><i style={{ width: `${completed / items.length * 100}%` }}/></div></header>
    {finished ? <div className="sr-results"><Check size={32}/><h2 ref={heading} tabIndex={-1}>You worked through the set.</h2><p>{completed} of {items.length} sentences completed. Your written explanations were self-reviewed, not automatically graded.</p><div className="sr-review">{items.map((q, i) => <button key={i} onClick={() => move(i)}>Sentence {i + 1}<ArrowRight size={16}/></button>)}</div></div> : <>
    <div className="sr-scene"><aside className="sr-statement"><span>SENTENCE {index + 1} OF {items.length}</span><blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote><div className="sr-mini-arrow" aria-hidden="true"><span>Enough</span><b>→</b><span>Required</span></div></aside>
    <div className="sr-work"><ol className="sr-stages" aria-label="Steps for this sentence">{item.stages.map((s, i) => <li key={i} aria-current={step === i ? 'step' : undefined} data-done={answers[`${index}:${i}`]?.accepted}><span>{answers[`${index}:${i}`]?.accepted ? <Check size={13}/> : i + 1}</span>{s.name}</li>)}</ol>
    <div className="sr-active" key={key}><p className="sr-step-label">STEP {step + 1} OF {item.stages.length}</p><h2 ref={heading} tabIndex={-1}>{stage.prompt}</h2>
    {objective ? <div className="sr-options" role="group" aria-label="Answer choices">{stage.options!.map((option, i) => <button key={i} aria-pressed={answer.choice === i} disabled={answer.checked} onClick={() => update({ choice: i, checked: false, accepted: false })}><span>{String.fromCharCode(65 + i)}</span>{option}</button>)}</div> : <label className="sr-writing">Your explanation<textarea rows={4} value={answer.writing} disabled={answer.checked} onChange={e => update({ writing: e.target.value, accepted: false })}/></label>}
    {!answer.checked ? <button className="sr-primary" disabled={objective ? answer.choice === null : !answer.writing.trim()} onClick={() => update({ checked: true, accepted: objective && correct })}>{objective ? 'Check this step' : 'Compare my explanation'}<ArrowRight size={17}/></button> : <div className="sr-feedback"><h3 ref={feedback} tabIndex={-1}>{objective ? correct ? rightHeading(stage.name) : 'Not quite. Try again.' : 'Compare the meaning.'}</h3>{stage.model && <p className="sr-model">{stage.model}</p>}<p>{stage.explanation}</p>{!objective && <button className="sr-self-check" aria-pressed={answer.accepted} onClick={() => update({ accepted: !answer.accepted })}><Check size={16}/>I compared the direction and complete conditions</button>}<button className="sr-text" onClick={() => update({ checked: false, accepted: false })}>Revise my answer</button></div>}
    </div></div></div>
    <nav className="sr-navigation" aria-label="Practice navigation"><button disabled={index === 0 && step === 0} onClick={() => step ? setStep(step - 1) : move(index - 1)}><ArrowLeft size={16}/>Previous</button><button className="sr-primary" disabled={!answer.accepted} onClick={next}>{step < item.stages.length - 1 ? 'Next step' : index < items.length - 1 ? 'Next sentence' : 'Review this set'}<ArrowRight size={16}/></button></nav></>}
  </section>;
}

const drillInstructions: Record<string, { summary: string; heading: string; steps: string[] }> = {
  'sufficient-practice': {
    summary: 'Condition groups and diagrams', heading: 'Find the whole sufficient condition',
    steps: ['Find the complete idea the sufficient indicator refers to.', 'Put that condition on the left and the required condition on the right. Read the arrow back in English.', 'Check your work, then build a possible case that shows why the reverse does not follow.'],
  },
  'contrapositive-practice': {
    summary: 'Originals and contrapositives', heading: 'Check the direction and each negation',
    steps: ['Choose the original arrow that keeps both complete conditions.', 'Choose its contrapositive: reverse the sides and negate both conditions. Correct each choice before continuing.', 'Write each form in ordinary English, then compare your wording with the explanation.'],
  },
};

function DrillEntry({ step, url, storageScope }: { step: OpeningCourseStep; url: OpeningCourseUrl; storageScope?: string }) {
  if (getSourceWorksheetDrill(step.id)) return <SourceWorksheetDrill setId={step.id} title={step.title} run={false} startUrl={url(step.id, true)} storageScope={storageScope}/>;
  if (step.id === 'foundation-practice') return <DrillOneEntry startUrl={url(step.id, true)} lessonsUrl={url('lessons')}/>;
  if (step.id === 'mixed-practice') return <div className="oc-drill-landing oc-translation-entry">
    <div className="oc-drill-overview">
      <Link className="oc-back oc-entry-back" to={url('lessons')}><ArrowLeft size={16}/>Back to lessons</Link>
      <header data-course-heading><p className="oc-label">Conditional reasoning</p><h1>{step.title}</h1><p>{step.description}</p></header>
    </div>
    <section className="oc-drill-start" aria-label="Practice instructions">
      <h2>One step at a time</h2>
      <p>Before each section, we'll show you what to look for. Then you'll put it into practice.</p>
      <Link className="oc-action oc-start-pulse" to={url(step.id, true)}>Start practice<ArrowRight size={16}/></Link>

## assistant

I made both rounds of changes and pushed them to [PR #204](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/204) as two new commits. All 303 bootcamp tests pass, and the type check and build both pass. I checked the drill in the browser on desktop and at phone width.

**The wording sweep:**
- **What changed:**
  - "expression" became "indicator", "phrase" or "condition", depending on what it meant.
  - "establish" became "prove", "preserve" became "keeps", and "relationship" became "rule".
  - "the model" became "our version", and "Personalized If/Then" became "Put yourself in it".
  - "target" became "what it points to" or "the group".
- **What I protected:**
  - I didn't touch question prompts or answer options. A test fingerprints the opening questions as the record behind students' saved choices and diagrams, so editing them could break completed work.
  - Your worksheet sentences stay exactly as written.
  - The consequence: the old words are still in those question banks, about 30 rows in the applications set alone. Changing them needs a safe migration, so it's a follow-up.
- **The rules are logged.** They're in the word table in [COPY-CORRECTIONS.md](wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md), with the reason for each. Your interview answers are in [the capture file](brainstorms/2026-10-11-student-copy-word-preferences.md).

**The drill redesign:**
- **Counter:** a large "01 / 24" numeral that animates when the sentence changes, in place of "SENTENCE 1 OF 24".
- **Right answer:** the option turns green with a check mark, and the feedback panel is green with a heading for that step, such as "Right indicator." A "Continue" button appears inside the feedback. The other options fade back.
- **Wrong answer:** the option turns red with an X and a short shake. The panel is red and says "Not quite. Try again."
- **Before checking:** your choice shows in neutral blue. It was green before, which looked like a right answer.
- **Motion:** each step slides in, the track between steps fills as you go, and the page scrolls smoothly to the feedback.
- **Arrows:** I removed the decorative "Enough → Required" strip and the arrow icons on the buttons and "Back to instructions".
- **Legend:** in its place, the dark panel now reads "Sufficient: guarantees the result / Necessary: has to be true for it". You said you want to review this wording.
- **Instructions:** they listed three steps that didn't match the drill. They now describe its four real steps.

**Not done yet:**
- **The contrapositive drill:** it uses the same component, so it gets the new design too. I only looked at the sufficient drill in the browser.
- **Wrong-answer feedback gives away the answer.** It shows the same explanation as a right answer, for example "Read 'if' with the complete condition…". It should give a hint instead. This is a content change across all 24 items.
- **I kept the step named "Build the arrow".** That step is the diagram itself, so I read "no arrows" as meaning the decorative ones. Tell me if you meant the name too.
- **Other bootcamps (Main Conclusion, Flaw):** they still use these words. I only swept the conditionals bootcamp.

## user

type Stage = { name: string; prompt: string; options?: string[]; correct?: number; explanation: string; model?: string };
type Item = { sentence: string; stages: Stage[] };
type Answer = { choice: number | null; writing: string; checked: boolean; accepted: boolean };
const empty = (): Answer => ({ choice: null, writing: '', checked: false, accepted: false });
const not = (text: string, negative?: boolean) => negative ? `NOT (${text})` : text;
const RIGHT: Record<string, string> = { 'Find the indicator': 'Right indicator.', 'Find the condition': 'Right condition.', 'Build the arrow': 'Right direction.', 'Reverse and negate': 'Right contrapositive.', 'Original rule': 'Right rule.' };
function rightHeading(name: string) { return RIGHT[name] ?? "That's right."; }
function choices(name: string, prompt: string, options: string[], correct: number, explanation: string): Stage { return { name, prompt, options, correct, explanation }; }
function reading(model: string, explanation: string): Stage { return { name: 'Write it in English', prompt: 'Read your arrow as an if/then sentence.', explanation, model }; }

export const sufficientStagedItems: Item[] = [
  ...[...translationExamples['group-1'], ...diagramExamples['group-1'], ...flashcardExamples['group-1']].map((item, index) => {

      choices('Find the condition', `What complete condition does “${item.word}” identify?`, options, options.indexOf(item.left), item.why),
      choices('Build the arrow', 'Which direction keeps the original rule?', index % 2 ? [`${item.right} → ${item.left}`, `${item.left} → ${item.right}`] : [`${item.left} → ${item.right}`, `${item.right} → ${item.left}`], index % 2, item.why),
      reading(item.readback, 'Compare the condition on each side. Keep every restriction and do not turn the requirement into a guarantee.'),
    ] };

];

export const contrapositiveStagedItems: Item[] = referencePracticeItems.map((item, index) => {
  const left = not(item.left, item.leftNegated), right = not(item.right, item.rightNegated);
  const notLeft = not(item.left, !item.leftNegated), notRight = not(item.right, !item.rightNegated);
  return { sentence: item.sentence, stages: [

    choices('Reverse and negate', 'Which diagram is the contrapositive?', index % 2 ? [`${notRight} → ${notLeft}`, `${right} → ${left}`, `${notLeft} → ${notRight}`] : [`${right} → ${left}`, `${notRight} → ${notLeft}`, `${notLeft} → ${notRight}`], index % 2 ? 0 : 1, 'Reverse the conditions and negate each whole condition. ## assistant

I fixed the wrong-answer feedback so it gives a hint, reworded the legend, and finished the rest of the list. It's all pushed to [PR #204](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/204). All 303 bootcamp tests pass and the type check is clean. I didn't re-run the build after these last two commits.

**Wrong answers now get a hint.** The hint says why the chosen answer fails, without naming the right one. I checked these in the browser:
- **Picking "only if" on the indicator step:** "“only if” introduces what is required, not what is enough. Look for the word that introduces the guarantee."
- **Contrapositive drill, reversed but not negated:** "You reversed the sides but did not negate them. A contrapositive also negates both."
- **Contrapositive drill, negations changed:** "That changes the negations. Keep each condition exactly as the sentence states it."

Every other step has its own hint too. The full explanation still appears once the student gets it right.

**Legend:** it now reads "Sufficient: enough to guarantee the result" and "Necessary: required for the result". You were right that "it" had nothing to refer to.

**Contrapositive drill:** I checked it in the browser. The "01 / 9" counter and the green and red states work there too.

**Question banks:** these were the part I held back last time. They now follow the same word rules.
- I confirmed that all 326 questions keep the same IDs, option counts and answer keys, so students' saved answers are unaffected.
- In questions, "established" became "proven" ("Credit is not proven."), because "proved" read badly there.
- "The model" stays in the parallel-reasoning questions, where it means the model argument.
- One guard test fingerprinted the full question wording. It now fingerprints only what saved answers depend on: IDs, option order and answer keys. Rewording will no longer trip it, but a change to any of those still will.

**Not done: the other bootcamps.** Main Conclusion, Principle/Flaw, Flaw and RC have about 100 uses of these words. I left them alone for two reasons:
- Many are inside actual LSAT passages and stimuli, which can't be edited.
- In LR, "establish" has its own meaning. Premises don't "prove" a conclusion, so swapping in "prove" would teach the wrong idea there.

If you want those bootcamps reviewed, it should be a separate pass with its own rules.

I added two rows to the word table in [COPY-CORRECTIONS.md](wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md): labels must make sense on their own, and wrong-answer feedback gives a hint, not the answer.

One question from last time is still open: should the step called "Build the arrow" keep that name?

import type { ConditionalDifficulty, ConditionalUnit } from './content/types';
import { getCurriculumDifficulty, getCurriculumUnits } from './curriculumPath';
import { conditionalOpeningEditionKey, isConditionalLessonEditionKey, openingQuestionRevisions } from './openingRevision';

export interface AnswerDraft {
  working: string;
  hint: boolean;
  revealed: boolean;
  choice: number | null;
  checked: boolean;
}
export interface ConditionalAttempt {
  id: string;
  questionId: string;
  unitId: string;
  difficulty: ConditionalDifficulty;
  choice: number | null;
  correct: boolean;
  hint: boolean;
  explanationBeforeCheck: boolean;
  working: string;
  checkedAt: string;
}
export interface ConditionalRun {
  id: string;
  questionIds: string[];
  index: number;
  drafts: Record<string, AnswerDraft>;
  finished: boolean;
  elapsed: number;
  timerEnabled: boolean;
  timerStartedAt?: string | null;
}
export interface ConditionalProgress {
  id: 'conditionals-v1';
  lessons: Record<string, string>;
  runs: Record<string, ConditionalRun>;
  attempts: ConditionalAttempt[];
  journal: string;
  settings: { showMethod: boolean };
  updated_at: string;
}
export const emptyDraft = (): AnswerDraft => ({ working: '', hint: false, revealed: false, choice: null, checked: false });
export const emptyProgress = (): ConditionalProgress => ({ id: 'conditionals-v1', lessons: {}, runs: {}, attempts: [], journal: '', settings: { showMethod: true }, updated_at: new Date().toISOString() });
export function markConditionalLessonRead(progress: ConditionalProgress, unitId: string, readAt = new Date().toISOString()): ConditionalProgress {
  const editionKey = conditionalOpeningEditionKey(unitId);
  return { ...progress, lessons: { ...progress.lessons, [unitId]: readAt, ...(editionKey ? { [editionKey]: readAt } : {}) } };
}
export function reviewedConditionalLessonCount(lessons: Record<string, string>): number {
  return Object.keys(lessons).filter(id => !isConditionalLessonEditionKey(id)).length;
}
export function newRun(questionIds: string[]): ConditionalRun {
  return { id: crypto.randomUUID(), questionIds, index: 0, drafts: {}, finished: false, elapsed: 0, timerEnabled: false };
}
function hasCheckedQuestion(run: ConditionalRun, questionId: string, attempts: ConditionalAttempt[]): boolean {
  return !!run.drafts[questionId]?.checked || attempts.some(attempt => attempt.questionId === questionId && attempt.id.startsWith(`${run.id}:`));
}
export function nextUncheckedQuestionIndex(run: ConditionalRun, attempts: ConditionalAttempt[], after = -1): number {
  const pending = (id: string) => !hasCheckedQuestion(run, id, attempts);
  const next = run.questionIds.findIndex((id, index) => index > after && pending(id));

export function reconcileConditionalRun(run: ConditionalRun, questions: { id: string; unitId: string; difficulty: ConditionalDifficulty }[], attempts: ConditionalAttempt[]): ConditionalRun {
  const savedIds = new Set(run.questionIds);
  const removedIds = new Set<string>();
  const addedIds: string[] = [];
  for (const revision of openingQuestionRevisions) {
    if (![...revision.previousIds, ...revision.addedIds].some(id => savedIds.has(id))) continue;
    const allowedIds = new Set(questions.filter(question => question.unitId === revision.unitId && question.difficulty === revision.difficulty).map(question => question.id));
    for (const [retiredId, replacementId] of Object.entries(revision.replacements)) {
      if (savedIds.has(retiredId) && allowedIds.has(replacementId)) removedIds.add(retiredId);
    }
    for (const id of revision.addedIds) if (allowedIds.has(id) && !savedIds.has(id)) addedIds.push(id);
  }
  if (!removedIds.size && !addedIds.length) return run;
  const questionIds = [...run.questionIds.filter(id => !removedIds.has(id)), ...addedIds];
  const currentId = run.questionIds[run.index];
  const currentIndex = questionIds.indexOf(currentId);
  const keepCurrent = !run.finished && currentIndex >= 0 && questions.some(question => question.id === currentId);
  const updated = { ...run, questionIds };
  const pendingIndex = nextUncheckedQuestionIndex(updated, attempts);
  const index = keepCurrent ? currentIndex : pendingIndex >= 0 ? pendingIndex : questionIds.length;
  return { ...updated, index, finished: !keepCurrent && pendingIndex < 0 };
}
export function attemptSummary(attempts: ConditionalAttempt[]) {
  return { total: attempts.length, correct: attempts.filter(a => a.correct).length, assisted: attempts.filter(a => a.hint || a.explanationBeforeCheck).length };
}
export function parseConditionalProgress(value: unknown): ConditionalProgress {
  const bad = () => { throw new Error('Your stored conditional progress has an unsupported format. Your record has been kept; ask Joshua to review it.'); };
  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object') return bad();
  const record = value as ConditionalProgress;
  if (record.id !== 'conditionals-v1' || !record.lessons || typeof record.lessons !== 'object' || !record.runs || typeof record.runs !== 'object' || !Array.isArray(record.attempts) || typeof record.journal !== 'string' || typeof record.settings?.showMethod !== 'boolean' || !Number.isFinite(Date.parse(record.updated_at))) return 
  if (Object.values(record.lessons).some(date => typeof date !== 'string' || !Number.isFinite(Date.parse(date)))) return bad();
  for (const run of Object.values(record.runs)) {
    if (!run || typeof run.id !== 'string' || !Array.isArray(run.questionIds) || run.questionIds.some(id => typeof id !== 'string') || !Number.isInteger(run.index) || run.index < 0 || run.index > run.questionIds.length || !run.drafts || typeof run.drafts !== 'object' || typeof run.finished !== 'boolean' || typeof run.timerEnable
    for (const draft of Object.values(run.drafts)) if (!draft || typeof draft.working !== 'string' || typeof draft.hint !== 'boolean' || typeof draft.revealed !== 'boolean' || typeof draft.checked !== 'boolean' || (draft.choice !== null && (!Number.isInteger(draft.choice) || draft.choice < 0))) return bad();
  }
  for (const attempt of record.attempts) if (!attempt || typeof attempt.id !== 'string' || typeof attempt.questionId !== 'string' || typeof attempt.unitId !== 'string' || !['beginner', 'medium', 'advanced'].includes(attempt.difficulty) || typeof attempt.correct !== 'boolean' || typeof attempt.hint !== 'boolean' || typeof attempt
  return record;
}
export function elapsedSeconds(run: ConditionalRun, now = Date.now()): number {
  return run.elapsed + (run.timerStartedAt ? Math.max(0, Math.floor((now - Date.parse(run.timerStartedAt)) / 1000)) : 0);
}
export function recommendNext(progress: ConditionalProgress, units: ConditionalUnit[], startingUnitId?: string) {
  for (const unit of getCurriculumUnits(units, startingUnitId)) {
    const difficulty = getCurriculumDifficulty(unit);
    const phase = unit.phase;
    if (!progress.lessons[unit.id]) return { mode: 'learn' as const, unitId: unit.id, label: `Learn: ${unit.title}`, difficulty, phase };
    if (!progress.attempts.some(attempt => attempt.questionId === unit.guidedExample.id)) return { mode: 'learn' as const, unitId: unit.id, label: `Guided example: ${unit.title}`, difficulty, phase };
    if (unit.drills[difficulty].some(question => !progress.attempts.some(attempt => attempt.questionId === question.id && attempt.difficulty === difficulty))) {
      return { mode: 'practice' as const, unitId: unit.id, label: `Skill drill: ${unit.title}`, difficulty, phase };
    }
  }
  return null;
}
export function useConditionalProgress(userId: string) {
  const [progress, setProgress] = useState<ConditionalProgress | null>(null);
  const [savedProgress, setSavedProgress] = useState<ConditionalProgress | null>(null);
  const [status, setStatus] = useState<'loading' | 'unsaved' | 'saving' | 'saved' | 'error'>('loading');
  const [error, setError] = useState('');
  const [loadedActor, setLoadedActor] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const current = useRef<ConditionalProgress | null>(null);
  const revision = useRef(0);
  const persistedRevision = useRef(0);
  const generation = useRef(0);
  const controller = useRef<AbortController | null>(null);
  const inFlight = useRef(false);
  const localQueue = useRef(Promise.resolve());
  const flushRef = useRef<() => Promise<void>>();
  const cloudRevision = useRef<string | null>(null);

  const flush = useCallback(async () => {
    if (inFlight.current || !current.current || revision.current === persistedRevision.current) return;
    const actor = generation.current;
    inFlight.current = true;
    setStatus('saving');
    try {
      while (actor === generation.current && revision.current !== persistedRevision.current) {
        const version = revision.current;
        const snapshot = current.current;
        const writer = cloudRevision.current
          ? supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').update({ data: snapshot as never }).eq('user_id', userId).eq('table_name', TABLE).eq('record_id', RECORD).eq('updated_at', cloudRevision.current)
          : supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').insert({ user_id: userId, table_name: TABLE, record_id: RECORD, data: snapshot as never });
        const { data: acknowledgement, error: saveError } = await writer.select('record_id,updated_at').abortSignal(controller.current.signal).maybeSingle();
        if (saveError) {
          if (saveError.code === '23505') throw new Error('Another window saved this course first. Your draft is kept on this device; ask Joshua to reconcile the two versions.');
          throw saveError;
        }
        if (acknowledgement?.record_id !== RECORD || !acknowledgement.updated_at) throw new Error('Your saved work changed in another window. This draft is kept on this device. Reload to reconcile before continuing; it will not overwrite the other window.');
        if (actor !== generation.current) return;
        const acknowledgedBase = cloudRevision.current;
        cloudRevision.current = acknowledgement.updated_at;
        // New edits queued while this snapshot was in flight still have its old
        // base. Advance only that base after the acknowledgement, preserving the
        // newest draft contents and the order of any subsequent local writes.
        const nextBase = acknowledgement.updated_at;
        localQueue.current = localQueue.current.catch(() => undefined).then(async () => {
          if (actor !== generation.current) return;
          const base = await localStore(userId).get<{ revision: string | null }>('conditional_draft_base', RECORD);
          if (base?.revision === acknowledgedBase) {
            await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_base', RECORD, { revision: nextBase });
          }
        });
        await localQueue.current;
        if (actor !== generation.current) return;
        await localStore(userId).set(TABLE, RECORD, snapshot);
        if (actor !== generation.current) return;
        persistedRevision.current = version;
        setSavedProgress(snapshot);
      }
      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('saved'); setError(''); }
    } catch (failure) {
      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('error'); setError(failure instanceof Error ? failure.message : 'Your changes have not been saved. Retry while this page stays open.'); }
    } finally {
      if (actor === generation.current) inFlight.current = false;
    }
  }, [userId]);
  flushRef.current = flush;

  useEffect(() => {
    const actor = ++generation.current;
    const actorController = new AbortController();
    controller.current = actorController;
    current.current = null;
    revision.current = 0;
    persistedRevision.current = 0;
    inFlight.current = false;
    setProgress(null);
    setSavedProgress(null);
    setLoadedActor(null);
    setStatus('loading');
    void (async () => {
      try {
        const persistence = localStore(userId);
        await persistence.ready();
        // A failed initial cloud read must not manufacture defaults over account progress.
        const { data, error: readError } = await supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').select('data,updated_at').eq('user_id', userId).eq('table_name', TABLE).eq('record_id', RECORD).abortSignal(actorController.signal).maybeSingle();
        if (readError) throw readError;
        const saved = data?.data != null ? parseConditionalProgress(data.data) : undefined;
        const rawLocal = await persistence.get<unknown>('conditional_draft_state', RECORD);
        const local = rawLocal != null ? parseConditionalProgress(rawLocal) : null;
        const dirty = local && (!saved || Date.parse(local.updated_at) > Date.parse(saved.updated_at));
        const draftBase = await persistence.get<{ revision: string | null }>('conditional_draft_base', RECORD);
        if (dirty && draftBase && draftBase.revision !== (data?.updated_at ?? null)) throw new Error('This device has unfinished work based on an older account version. Both versions are kept. Ask Joshua to reconcile them before continuing.');
        const value = dirty ? local : saved ?? emptyProgress();
        if (actor !== generation.current) return;
        current.current = value;
        cloudRevision.current = data?.updated_at ?? null;
        setProgress(value);
        setSavedProgress(saved ?? emptyProgress());
        setLoadedActor(userId);
        if (dirty) revision.current = 1;
        setStatus(dirty ? 'unsaved' : 'saved');
      } catch (failure) {
        if (actor === generation.current) { setLoadedActor(userId); setStatus('error'); setError(failure instanceof Error ? failure.message : 'Could not load your saved work. Reload to try again.'); }
      }
    })();
    return () => { generation.current = actor + 1; actorController.abort(); };
  }, [userId]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (status !== 'unsaved') return;
    const timer = setTimeout(() => void flushRef.current?.(), 350);
    return () => clearTimeout(timer);
  }, [progress, status]);

  useEffect(() => {
    const beforeUnload = (event: BeforeUnloadEvent) => {
      if (revision.current === persistedRevision.current) return;
      event.preventDefault();
      event.returnValue = '';
    };
    window.addEventListener('beforeunload', beforeUnload);
    return () => window.removeEventListener('beforeunload', beforeUnload);
  }, []);

  const update = useCallback((transform: (previous: ConditionalProgress) => ConditionalProgress) => {
    if (!current.current || loadedActor !== userId) return;
    const value = { ...transform(current.current), updated_at: new Date().toISOString() };
    current.current = value;
    revision.current++;
    setProgress(value);
    setStatus(inFlight.current ? 'saving' : 'unsaved');
    const actor = generation.current;
    const baseRevision = cloudRevision.current;
    localQueue.current = localQueue.current.catch(() => undefined).then(async () => {
      await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_state', RECORD, value);
      await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_base', RECORD, { revision: baseRevision });
    }).catch(() => {
      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('error'); setError('Could not keep this draft on your device. Keep this page open and retry saving.'); }
    });
  }, [userId, loadedActor]);
  return { progress: loadedActor === userId ? progress : null, savedProgress: loadedActor === userId ? savedProgress : null, update, status: loadedActor === userId ? status : 'loading' as const, error: loadedActor === userId ? error : '', retry: flush };
}

function OpeningCourseRoute({ view: surface }: { view?: string }) {
  const { stepId } = useParams();
  const location = useLocation();
  const [params] = useSearchParams();
  const { isAdmin, active, selection, userId } = useWork();
  const steps = OPENING_COURSE_PATH.filter(step => isAdmin || !!active && openingStepAccessUnits(step).some(unitId => conditionalAssignmentAllowsAction(active, {
    unitId, action: step.type === 'note' ? 'lesson' : 'guided', difficulty: active.difficulty ?? getCurriculumDifficulty(unitId),
  })));
  const units = selectedUnits(selection).filter(unit => CONDITIONAL_OPENING_UNIT_IDS.some(id => id === unit.id));
  const recordedPractice = <section className="cb-card"><h2>Your assigned skill drills</h2><p>These drills record your responses and reasoning in your existing work.</p><div className="cb-actions">{units.filter(unit => isAdmin || !!active && conditionalAssignmentAllowsAction(active, { unitId: unit.i
  return <OpeningCourseScreen view={surface ?? stepId} run={params.get('run') === '1'} url={courseUrl(location.search)} steps={steps} recordedPractice={recordedPractice} storageScope={userId}/>;
}

Returns null outside a course (isolated\n * component tests), where drills fall back to their own device storage.\n */\nexport function useCourseStep(stepId: string | undefined) {\n  const store = useContext(CourseProgressContext);\n  const save = store?.save;\n  const record = useCallback((patch: CourseStepPatch) => { if (stepId) save?.(stepId, patch); }, [save, stepId]);\n  if (!store || !stepId) return null;\n  return { saved: store.steps[stepId], record };\n}\n\n/** Device-only store for the preview harness and signed-out use; the course route uses the account. */\nexport function useDeviceCourseProgress(scope = 'preview'): CourseProgressStore {\n  const key = `conditional-course-progress:${scope}`;\n  const [steps, setSteps] = useState<Record<string, CourseStepProgress>>(() => {\n    try { return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(key) ?? export function SourceWorksheetDrill({ setId, run = false, startUrl, title, storageScope = 'preview' }: { setId: string; run?: boolean; startUrl?: string; title?: string; storageScope?: string }) {
  const set = getSourceWorksheetDrill(setId);
  if (!set) return <p>This practice set is unavailable.</p>;
  return <div className="opening-course-content"><WorksheetRun key={`${storageScope}-${setId}-${run}`} set={set} title={title} run={run} startUrl={startUrl} storageScope={storageScope}/></div>;
}

function WorksheetRun({ set, run, startUrl, title, storageScope }: { set: SourceWorksheetDrillSet; run: boolean; startUrl?: string; title?: string; storageScope: string }) {
  // The unscoped preview key belongs to the local dev preview. Signed-in routes pass their user id, so students on a shared device never share drafts.
  const storageKey = storageScope === 'preview' ? `conditional-worksheet-v1:${set.id}` : `conditional-worksheet-v1:${storageScope}:${set.id}`;
  const stages = stagesFor(set.taskKind);
  const stageName = (value: Stage) => set.taskKind === 'rephrase' && value === 'readback' ? 'Rewrite the rule' : names[value];
  const [saved, setSaved] = useState(() => {
    const restored = readSaved(storageKey);
    for (const item of set.items) {
      const a = restored.answers[item.id];
      if (a && (a.fingerprint !== fingerprint(item) || !a.passed.length)) {
        // Keep earlier drafts. The new staged workflow asks the learner to check each operation again.
        a.revealed = false; a.review = null; a.stage = 0; a.passed = [];
      }
    }
    return restored;
  });
  const [started, setStarted] = useState(run);
  const [results, setResults] = useState(false);
  const [storageFailed, setStorageFailed] = useState(false);
  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);
  const heading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);
  const feedback = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);
  const current = Math.max(0, Math.min(saved.current, set.items.length - 1));
  const item = set.items[current];
  const answer = saved.answers[item.id] ?? blank();
  const stageIndex = Math.max(0, Math.min(answer.stage, stages.length - 1));

type Question =
  | { id: string; stage: number; kind: 'choice'; item: MixedDrillChoiceItem }

  | { id: string; stage: number; kind: 'independent'; item: (typeof mixedFamilyItems)[number]; itemIndex: number };

const baselineQuestions: Question[] = [
  ...completeConditionItems.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 0, kind: 'choice' as const, item })),
  ...openingActivityItems.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 1, kind: 'diagram' as const, item })),

  ...equivalenceItems.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 3, kind: 'choice' as const, item })),
  ...transferItems.map(item => ({ id: item.id, stage: 4, kind: 'choice' as const, item })),
];

};
type Result = { firstCorrect: boolean; correct: boolean; errors: MixedDrillError[] };
type Check = (correct: boolean | null, errors?: MixedDrillError[]) => void;

it('keeps assignment context without recording legacy completion on new course navigation', () => {
  mount(`${base}/opening/necessary-words?assignment=skill&level=advanced&path=skill&start=group-2`, [skill()]);
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('link', { name: /Next lesson Keep the whole meaning/ }));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('link', { name: /Next drill Drill 2: Find both conditions/ }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'Drill 2: Find both conditions' })).toBeVisible();
  const next = screen.getByRole('link', { name: /Start practice/ }).getAttribute('href')!;
  expect(next).toContain('assignment=skill');
  expect(next).toContain('level=advanced');
  expect(next).toContain('start=group-2');
  expect(state.updateCalls).toBe(0);
  expect(state.progress!.lessons).toEqual({});
});


it('keeps orientation-only contraposition material out of a sufficient-necessary skill assignment', () => {
  const assigned: ResolvedConditionalAssignment = { ...skill(), id: 'roles', title: 'Enough and required', target: { scope: 'skill', mode: 'learn', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', difficulty: 'beginner' }, unitIds: ['sufficient-necessary'], difficulty: 'beginn
  mount(`${base}/opening/contrapositive?assignment=roles`, [assigned]);
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'This lesson is not available here.' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(state.updateCalls).toBe(0);
});


it('keeps root-entry query context when opening the revised Lessons index', () => {
  mount(`${base}?assignment=skill&level=advanced&path=skill&start=group-2&unit=group-2`, [skill()]);
  const entry = screen.getByRole('link', { name: 'Begin lesson' }).getAttribute('href')!;
  for (const field of ['assignment=skill', 'level=advanced', 'path=skill', 'start=group-2', 'unit=group-2']) expect(entry).toContain(field);
});

it.each(['/lessons', '/opening/mixed-practice?run=1', '/opening-practice', '/opening-labs', '/opening-tools'])('scopes account saving to assigned work on the local opening surface %s', path => {
  mount(`${base}${path}`);
  expect(screen.getByText('Assigned work saved to your account')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByText('Saved to your account')).toBeNull();
  expect(state.updateCalls).toBe(0);
});

it('explains local mixed practice without recording it as assigned work', () => {
  mount(`${base}/opening/mixed-practice?run=1`);
  expect(screen.getByText("Practice answers aren't saved to your account. Reloading or leaving the course clears them.")).toBeVisible();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Begin practice' }));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Every swimmer training before dawn' }));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer' }));
  expect(state.updateCalls).toBe(0);
  expect(state.progress!.attempts).toEqual([]);
});

it.each(['/lessons/rules-facts', '/practice?unit=group-2'])('keeps account saving for recorded work on %s', path => {
  mount(`${base}${path}`);
  expect(screen.getByText('Saved to your account')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByText('Assigned work saved to your account')).toBeNull();
});

it('keeps pending account changes visible on an opening surface', () => {
  state.status = 'unsaved';
  mount(`${base}/opening/mixed-practice`);
  expect(screen.getByText('Assigned work waiting to save')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByText('Assigned work saved to your account')).toBeNull();
});

vi.mock('./useConditionalProgress', async () => {
  const { useCallback, useReducer } = await import('react');
  return { useConditionalProgress: () => {
    const [, refresh] = useReducer((count: number) => count + 1, 0);
    const update = useCallback((transform: (previous: ConditionalProgress) => ConditionalProgress) => {
      state.updateCalls++;
      state.progress = transform(state.progress!);
      if (state.status === 'saved') state.saved = state.progress;
      refresh();
    }, []);
    return { progress: state.progress, savedProgress: state.saved, status: state.status, error: '', retry: vi.fn(), update };

## assistant

I removed the header line and built the progress saving and tracker. Both are pushed to [PR #204](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/204). All 306 bootcamp tests pass, three of them new, and the type check and build both pass.

**Header:** the "Learn one idea, use it…" line is gone. The Practice page had a line in the same style ("Make an attempt before reading the explanation…"), so I removed that too. The cards also said "NOTE", which is an internal word; they now say "Lesson".

**Saving.** Every lesson and drill now saves to the student's account. Before this, only "lesson read" was saved there. Drills 2 to 8 were saved only in that browser, and Drill 1 and mixed practice weren't saved at all.
- **Lessons** are marked "In progress" when opened and "Done" when the student reaches the end.
- **Every drill picks up where the student stopped,** on any device:
  - Spot the condition
  - Build the sufficient side
  - the contrapositive practice
  - Drills 2 to 8 and the two Revisit drills
  - mixed practice
- **Mixed practice resumes at the question, not part-way through one.** An answer that was picked but not yet checked is lost.
- **A finished drill stays finished,** even if the student goes back and redoes part of it.
- **Old student records are safe.** Progress goes into a new field on the account record the course already uses, and records saved before today load as normal. The separate record that drives assigned work and recommendations is untouched, and I updated the tests to check that.
- **The "Your answers aren't saved" notices are gone,** because they're no longer true.

**Tracker:**
- A bar at the top of the Lessons and Practice pages reads "N of 22 done" (13 on Practice), with one segment per step: grey for not started, amber for in progress, green for done.
- Each card shows "Not started", a count like "7 / 24" with a small bar, or "Done". A coloured left edge marks started and finished cards.
- The card button changes to "Continue" once a step is started, and "Review drill" or "Read again" once it's done.

I checked in the browser that opening a lesson marks it in progress, reaching the end marks it done, and a started drill shows as in progress on the Practice page. That was in the preview, which saves to the browser. I haven't tested saving to a real account; the tests use a mock account, and they pass.

**Worth knowing:**
- **The count only rises when a whole sentence is finished.** A drill shows "0 / 24" until the first sentence is fully done.
- **Old browser-only drill saves don't move over.** Saved drill state was carried into the account version, but anything a student saved in their browser before this change stays there. It's preview work, so nobody should lose anything real.

<result>I found five High issues across Drills 2–5. The worst: Drill 2 teaches "only after" without ever having taught it, and the indicator check marks a student wrong for selecting "who" in some sentences but requires it in another. I edited nothing in the repo. One slip on my side: I briefly copied a temporary script into `content/`, deleted it straight away, and `git status` shows the folder clean.

I re-checked every High finding against the source. Every answer key (sufficient/necessary, diagram, contrapositive) is correct. All indicator texts can be selected. The problems are in what gets taught, how answers are checked, and what feedback shows.

## Shared engine (affects all four drills)

**How indicator selection handles phrases.** The sentence is split on spaces and punctuation is stripped, so "if," and "Whenever," match. Adjacent selected words merge into one phrase, so "only if", "The only" and "Only after" can all be selected correctly. Two side effects:
- Any extra adjacent word fails the whole check. "Members who" is marked wrong because the key is just "who".
- Two indicators that sit next to each other could never be graded separately. No current item has this.

**Findings:**
- **Medium.** `SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:133`. Wrong-answer feedback gives the answer away. When the indicators step is wrong, the screen shows "Make one adjustment" but also prints `Indicators: …` with the full key and the explanation. Drill 4's placement step also prints `item.diagram` on a wrong answer.
  - Fix: on a wrong answer, show only the hint line. Reveal the key and explanation only when the answer is correct or the student asks to see it.
- **Medium.** `SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:134`. Each item's internal source notes are shown to students under "Source adaptation". They read like editing notes: "The source key conflicts over the classification of 'the only.'" (`content/sourceWorksheetDrills.ts:544`), "needs clarification before grading" (`:650`), "The key incorrectly turns…" (`:1373`), and "Reviewed derivative … remains in originalStatement" (e.g. `:72`).
  - Fix: hide these notes from students, or rewrite them for students.
- **Copy, Medium.** `SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:131`: "compare it with a model". Not reached in these four drills, but it breaks the copy rules.
  - Fix: "compare it with our version".

## Drill 2: source-intro-identify (steps: indicators, conditions, review)

**A. Flow**
- **Medium.** The course path title is "Find the indicator" (singular), but item 04 ("All managers must…", `content/sourceWorksheetDrills.ts:108`) needs two indicators: All (Group 1) and must (Group 2).
  - Fix: say "indicators" in the title and instructions, or tell students that some sentences have two.

**B. Item problems**
- **High: inconsistent "who".** Item 08 (`:220`) requires "who" as a Group 1 indicator. But in items 05 (`:140`, "any student who") and 13 (`:356`, "Every applicant who"), selecting "who" is marked wrong. Drill 3 items 02 and 13 have the same problem. The lesson teaches "people who" as a Group 1 indicator, so students will select it.
  - Fix: either accept "who" wherever it marks the condition, or replace item 08's key with something the lessons support. Also accept "Members who" as a phrase.
- **Medium.** Item 02 (`:56`) reads "you are instructed to expect thunder to follow". That is unnatural, and the key is built on "instructed".
  - Fix: "Whenever you see lightning, you should expect thunder to follow." The sufficient side becomes "you see lightning" and the necessary side "you should expect thunder."
- **Low.** Item 14 (`:394`, `:400`): "this fictional election" and "not election-law advice" read oddly.
  - Fix: "You must be over 18 to vote in the club election." Drop the disclaimer.
- **Low.** Item 12's explanation talks about "the left", but this drill has no arrow step yet.

**D. Preparation**
- **High: "Only after" is never taught.** Item 07 (`:194`) uses it. It is not one of the seven Group 2 indicators in `IndicatorNote` and appears in no word family.
  - Fix: add "only after" to the Group 2 indicator lesson (`necessary-words`), or rewrite the item with "only if".
- **Medium.** The Group 1 indicators (when/whenever, any, every, people who) show up in the course only as a linked list in the reference section on sufficient conditions. The full Group 1 indicator lesson (`IndicatorNote group="group-1"`) is never shown on the course path (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx:338-353`).
  - Fix: show the Group 1 lesson in the `sufficient-words` step.

**E. Copy**
- **Medium.** `content/sourceWorksheetDrills.ts:24`: "Select the indicator expressions… comparing with the model."
  - Fix: "Select the indicator words and their groups… before comparing with our version."
- **Medium.** `:71` "Preserve the expectation." and `:130` "Preserve the training requirement".
  - Fix: use "Keep".

## Drill 3: source-indicators (steps: indicators, review)

**A. Flow**
- **High: wrong order and repeated work.** Drill 3 is only the first step of Drill 2, on new sentences, and it comes after Drill 2. Students do something easier after something harder.
  - Fix: move Drill 3 before Drill 2, or fold its 13 sentences into Drill 2.

**B. Item problems**
- **Medium.** Item 11 (`:713`) is shown as "Errors are always documented before troubleshooting begins", which states a fact. Its note (`:731`) says it is an instruction and is not proof that anyone complied, so the note contradicts the sentence.
  - Fix: "Errors must always be documented before troubleshooting begins." That needs a "must" indicator added to the key.
- **Medium.** Item 04 (`:526`), "The only foods…", is keyed The only / Group 1, which is correct. But students then see the note "source key conflicts". This is covered under the shared engine; remove that note here.
- **Low.** Item 08 (`:632`) "Granting a loan requires collateral to be provided" is fine. Its note at `:650` needs to come out.

**D. Preparation**
- **High: "where" is never taught on the course path.** Item 05 (`:554`) uses "where" as Group 1. It is taught only in the Section 3 extension and in the Group 1 lessons, and neither is shown on the path.
  - Fix: show the Group 1 indicator lesson on the path, or cut this item.

**E. Copy**
- **Medium.** `:445`: "indicator expression… expressions… each expression".
  - Fix: use "indicator" or "word".

## Drill 4: source-roles (steps: conditions, placement, review)

**A. Flow**
- **Medium.** All 10 sentences repeat Drill 3's sentences. The placement step asks the student to pick one of two buttons, and those buttons show the answer key's wording right after the conditions step has displayed both conditions. That is very little challenge.
  - Fix: use new sentences, or make students place their own wording.
- **Low.** The path title "Place the conditions" and the set title "Identify each side of the rule" don't match.

**B. Item problems**
- All keys are correct.
- **Medium.** Item 10 (`:1462`) has the same "always" fact-versus-instruction problem as Drill 3 item 11.

**C. Feedback**
- **Medium.** The placement step reveals the full diagram after a wrong answer (covered under the shared engine).

**D. Preparation**
- All indicators used here are taught: only if, any, only when, must, all, requires, if, when, every, always.

## Drill 5: source-intro-diagrams (steps: conditions, symbols, diagram, review)

**A. Flow**
- **Medium.** These are the same 15 sentences as Drill 2, and the conditions step is done again. No step is auto-checked, so every step is self-compared. That is less checking than Drill 4, which comes before it.
  - Fix: skip the conditions step by carrying over the Drill 2 answers. Add an objective direction check, such as which symbol goes on the left.
- **Low.** The path title "Build the diagram" doesn't match the set title "Put the conditions beside the arrow".

**B. Item problems**
- Same as Drill 2: item 02 (`:833`, "instructed") and item 14 (`:1171`, "fictional election").

**D. Preparation**
- **High:** item 07 (`:971`) uses "Only after", which is never taught (same gap as Drill 2).

**E. Copy**
- `:848` and `:907` use "Preserve". Change both to "Keep".

<result>I found 4 High, 12 Medium and 8 Low issues across the three drills. The most common problem is wrong-answer feedback that gives away the answer, which shows up in all three drills. I made no edits. Paths below are relative to `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os-projects\lsatu-conditional-bootcamp\src\bootcamps\conditionals\`. I re-read the source for every High.

One routing note: `mixed-practice` goes to `StagedReasoningDrill mode="mixed"`, which only wraps `OpeningMixedDrill` (`StagedReasoningDrill.tsx:54`).

## 1. Spot the condition (`foundation-practice`)

There are 24 questions: 17 concept questions (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx:254-271`), then 7 built in `FoundationPractice` (`:283-297`).

**A. Flow**
- **Medium, difficulty goes backwards.** Questions 1-17 build up to "only if", "requires" and diagram choices. Then question 18 drops back to the easiest raffle question ("Does the rule itself tell us that you actually win the raffle?"), followed by the rain, café and delivery basics. Fix: move the 7 "rule vs fact" questions (`:283-287`) to just after the 5 concept questions.
- **Medium, terms taught inside a question.** "Trigger" and "required outcome" are defined only inside the prompts at `:260-261` (and on the entry page, `DrillOneExperience.tsx:30`). No note before this drill uses them. Fix: drop both terms and say "sufficient condition" and "necessary condition", or teach them in the `must-arrow` note.
- **Low.** "Try again" still appears after a correct answer (`DrillOneExperience.tsx:126`). Show it only when the answer was wrong.

**B. Sensibility**
- All 24 answer keys are correct.
- **Low.** The raffle question asks about "you", but its options are in first person: "Yes, it says I win." / "No, it tells me what follows if I win." (`ConditionalReferenceSequence.tsx:~216`). Its feedback also says "what getting one would prove", where "one" has nothing to refer to. Fix: "Yes, it says you win." and "what a win would prove".
- **Low.** The café question says "Today the café did not run out of bread", but answer A reads "It stayed open until the usual time". That is fine, but answer B's feedback, "Nothing here proves an early closing", nearly duplicates C's. Make each feedback distinct.

**C. Feedback**
- **High, answer revealed (checked twice).** After a wrong answer, the correct option gets a check icon (`DrillOneExperience.tsx:123`) and the text "The correct answer is marked above." appears (`:125`). This breaks the rule that wrong feedback hints. Fix: on a wrong answer, show only the hint and let the student try again. Reveal the answer only after a second miss, or not at all.
- **Medium, feedback that names the answer.** Several wrong-answer feedback strings state the correct answer outright:
  - `:271` option A: "Every identifies the sufficient group… Requires identifies what each one must have."
  - `:268` option B: "Only if identifies a requirement".
  - `:266` option A: "Every is the word that applies the guarantee"
  - `:269` option A: "Entry goes left".

  Rewrite each to explain why the chosen word fails, for example "Every points to who the rule covers, not to what is required."

**D. Preparation** (taught before this drill: meaning, sufficient, necessary, must-arrow, missing-requirement)
- **Medium, unverified.** "Every", "only if" and "requires" are tested in questions 11-17 (`:265-271`). They appear in the prose of reference sections 1-4. My search also covered section 3, which is not on the path, so I could not confirm which routed section teaches them. Check `ReferenceBody 2/4`.
- The contrapositive idea in the last question, a case that fits the rule but disproves the reversed claim (`:288`), is covered by `missing-requirement`.

**E. Copy**
- **Medium, numbered title.** "Drill &lt;em&gt;1&lt;/em&gt;" (`DrillOneExperience.tsx:28`) breaks the "Drill 1 as a title" rule. Fix: "Spot the condition".

---

## 2. Build the sufficient side (`sufficient-practice`) and Check your contrapositive (`contrapositive-practice`)

### Build the sufficient side
24 sentences, about 4 steps each. 21 come from a template (`StagedReasoningDrill.tsx:25-33`) and 3 are hand-written (`:34-36`).

**A. Flow**
- **Medium, same distractors every time.** Every "Find the indicator" step offers "only if" and "requires" as the wrong options (`:28`). After two sentences, students can pick by elimination without reading. Fix: draw distractors from the sentence itself, such as a qualifier word or the main verb.
- **Medium, too long.** Running 21 templated sentences through the same 4 steps is about 90 clicks. Fix: cut to about 10 sentences, one per indicator plus the 3 hand-written items.
- **Medium, sentences reused from the lesson.**
  - "A report is reviewed if it is submitted before noon." appears twice in this drill (template item 1 and `:34`), and it is also the lesson's own example (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx:83`).
  - "All registered guests receive a wristband." is the lesson's worked example.
  - "Any applicant who submits late pays a fee." is the lesson's check question.
  - "The only notebooks kept on this shelf are hardback." (`:35`) is also a lesson check question.

  Fix: drop the template copy of the report sentence and replace the reused ones with fresh sentences.
- **Medium, the writing step is mislabelled.** The "Write it in English" step asks "Read your arrow as an if/then sentence." (`:22`), but the box is labelled "Your explanation" (`:127`). Fix: prompt "Write your diagram as an if/then sentence." and label the box "Your if/then sentence".

**B. Sensibility**
- All template outputs read naturally ("Ask: the only what?", "Ask: where what?") and every key is correct.
- **Low.** The "Explain the limit" step (`:36`) sits after a question with four options. Option D's hint, "the claim says nothing about it", is correct.

**C. Feedback**
- Wrong answers show only the hint (`hintFor`), which is good.

**What needs your decision**

- **Spot the condition now requires the right answer before "Next question".** Wrong answers show a hint and "Try again", and nothing reveals the answer, so a student can't skip a missed question. Say if you'd rather let them move on after a miss.

**Saved work**

- **Spot the condition:** saved state now records an order version (`order: 2`). Older saves are moved to the new positions, so every saved answer stays with the question it answered. A test covers this.
- **Staged drills:** the old behaviour would have put old answers on the new sentences (an old written readback would prefill a different item's text box). Now, when the item set changes, the old answers are kept in the saved record under `archived` (last three sets) and the drill starts fresh. Finished drills stay marked done. A test covers this.
