**1. Spot the condition** (`DrillOneExperience.tsx`, `OpeningCourseContent.tsx`, `drill-one.css`)
- **Wrong answers:** no tick on the right option, "The correct answer is marked above" is removed, and the diagram readback only appears once the answer is right. "Try again" only shows after a wrong answer.
- **Order:** the 7 rule-vs-fact questions come first, then the 5 concept questions, then the 12 indicator questions.
- **Entry page:** the heading is now "Spot the condition". The three instruction lines are now:
  - "Start with rules and facts. Decide what a rule proves on its own, what a fact adds, and test a claim against a possible case."
  - "Recall what sufficient and necessary mean. Read the arrow and decide which condition belongs on each side."
  - "Find the indicator words and the complete conditions they introduce. Check each answer. If it isn't right, read the hint and try again."
- **The two "trigger" / "required outcome" prompts:**
  - "The sufficient condition is the one that is enough: when it is true, the other condition must be true. It does not have to cause the other condition or happen first. Which complete condition is sufficient here?"
  - "The necessary condition is what must be true when the sufficient condition holds. Which condition is necessary here?"
- **Raffle options** (`ConditionalReferenceSequence.tsx`, the same question appears in the lesson and the drill): "Yes, it says you win." / "No, it tells you what follows if you win."
- **Wrong-answer hints rewritten so they don't name the answer:**
  - A → B, "which goes left", option A: "The necessary condition is the one on the right: what must be true. Which role does the condition on the left play?"
  - Painting, option A: "An inventory number is what the rule says a painting must have. That makes it the necessary condition. Look for the condition that is enough to prove it."
  - Painting, option B (correct): "…Sufficient describes that logical role, not a cause or an earlier event."
  - Painting, option C: "If is an indicator, not a condition. Look for the complete idea it introduces."
  - Book, option A (correct): "The reservation card is necessary. A book on that shelf must have one. Necessary here does not mean an event that happens later."
  - Book, option B: "Being on the reserved shelf is what if introduces, so it is the condition that is enough. Look for what the rule says a book on that shelf must have."
  - Book, option C: "The rule says nothing about a later action. Look for what the rule says every book on the reserved shelf already has."
  - Application, option A: "Approval is what if introduces. That makes it the condition that is enough, and the right side holds what it requires."
  - Application, option C: "If is an indicator, not a condition. Put a complete idea beside the arrow."
  - Member, Must: "Must tells us what is required: having an active account. Look for the word that introduces the condition that is enough."
  - Member, Both: "The two words have different jobs. Only one of them introduces what is enough; the other marks what is required."
  - Volunteer, Must: "Must marks what is required: completed training. Look for the word that introduces the group the rule covers."
  - Volunteer, Wearing: "Wearing is part of the condition itself: it describes which volunteers the rule covers. An indicator is the word that tells you the rule applies to that whole group."
  - Document, If on its own: "If on its own would introduce what is enough. Here if does not stand alone. Read it together with the word right before it."
  - Document, Archived: "Archived is part of a condition: the document is archived. It is not an indicator. Look for the phrase that tells you what archiving requires."
  - Researcher, option A: "This arrow starts from having a permit. Could a researcher have a permit and still never enter the reading room? If so, the permit is not enough to prove entry."
  - Registration, Every: "Every introduces the group the rule covers: completed registrations. That is the sufficient side. Look for the word that tells you what each one must have."

**2. Build the sufficient side** (`StagedReasoningDrill.tsx`)
- **Size:** 13 sentences, down from 24: 10 indicator items plus 3 practice items. Each wrong indicator choice has its own hint, and the right answer's position varies.
- **The 10 indicator items** (sentence | readback | distractors):
  - **if:** "Mia takes the bus if it is raining." | "If it is raining, then Mia takes the bus." | only if, takes
  - **when:** "When the kettle boils, it clicks off." | "If the kettle boils, then it clicks off." | clicks, must
  - **whenever:** "Whenever Sam bakes bread, the kitchen smells of yeast." | "If Sam bakes bread, then the kitchen smells of yeast." | always, smells
  - **where:** "Where the sidewalk is icy, the city spreads salt." | "If a sidewalk is icy, then the city spreads salt there." | requires, icy
  - **all:** "All dogs at the park must be on a leash." | "If a dog is at the park, then it must be on a leash." | must, park
  - **every:** "Every guest at the wedding gets a slice of cake." | "If someone is a guest at the wedding, then that person gets a slice of cake." | gets, necessary
  - **each:** "Each player on the team gets a numbered jersey." | "If someone is a player on the team, then that person gets a numbered jersey." | team, only
  - **any:** "Any car parked in the fire lane gets towed." | "If a car is parked in the fire lane, then it gets towed." | need, towed
  - **the only:** "The only shoes allowed on the gym floor are sneakers." | "If shoes are allowed on the gym floor, then they are sneakers." | only, allowed
  - **people who:** "People who order the lunch special get a free drink." | "If a person orders the lunch special, then that person gets a free drink." | depends on, free
- **Each item's explanation:**
  - if: "If what? It is raining. The condition comes last in the sentence, but rain is enough to put Mia on the bus, so it goes left. Seeing Mia on the bus doesn't prove it is raining. She could take the bus on a dry day too."
  - when: "When what? The kettle boils. Boiling is enough for the click. A click alone doesn't prove the water boiled. Someone could switch the kettle off by hand."
  - whenever: "Whenever what? Sam bakes bread. Every time he bakes, the kitchen smells of yeast. The smell alone doesn't prove Sam baked. Someone else could be making pizza dough."
  - where: "Where what? The sidewalk is icy. Ice on a stretch of sidewalk is enough for salt there. Salt alone doesn't prove the sidewalk was icy. The city might salt a dry stretch before a storm."
  - all: "All what? Dogs at the park. Keep at the park. The rule says nothing about dogs at home. A dog on a leash could be anywhere, so the leash doesn't prove the dog is at the park."
  - every: "Every what? Guest at the wedding. Each one gets cake. Someone holding a slice could be a waiter, so the cake doesn't prove that person is a guest."
  - each: "Each what? Player on the team. Every single player gets a numbered jersey. A fan can buy a numbered jersey too, so the jersey doesn't prove someone plays."
  - any: "Any what? Car parked in the fire lane. Keep in the fire lane. The rule is not about every parked car. A towed car could have been parked somewhere else, so towing doesn't prove the fire lane."
  - the only: "The only what? Shoes allowed on the gym floor. Every pair allowed there is a pair of sneakers. The rule doesn't say every pair of sneakers is allowed. Muddy sneakers could still be kept off the floor."
  - people who: "People who what? Order the lunch special. Ordering it is enough for a free drink. A free drink doesn't prove someone ordered the special. It could be a birthday treat."
- **Hint templates:**
  - Group 2 distractor: "“X” introduces what is required, not what is enough. Look for the word that introduces the guarantee."
  - Word from the result: "“X” belongs to the result: …. Look for the word that introduces the condition that is enough."
  - Word from the group: "“X” is part of the group the rule covers. Look for the word that tells you the rule applies to that whole group."
- **Special hints:**
  - "the only" item, "only": "Only on its own marks a requirement. Here it comes right after “the,” and the whole phrase does a different job. Read the two words together."
  - "all" item, "must": "“Must” is in this sentence, but it marks what is required: being on a leash. Look for the word that introduces the group the rule covers."
  - Five items (all, every, each, any, the only) also offer a shortened condition as a wrong choice, with the hint "That drops part of the condition. Keep every word that limits it."
- **The three replacement practice items:**
  - **Same day, replaces the report sentence:** "A package ships the same day if it is ordered before 2 p.m."
    - Explanations: "Before 2 p.m. limits the rule. A package ordered at five does not meet the condition." / "Ordering before 2 p.m. is sufficient. Same-day shipping alone does not prove when the order came in."
    - Choices for "Which sentence says the same thing?": "If a package ships the same day, it was ordered before 2 p.m." / "If a package is ordered before 2 p.m., it ships the same day."
  - **Cookies, replaces the notebooks sentence:** "The only cookies left in the jar are oatmeal."
    - Explanation: "Every cookie left in the jar is oatmeal. An oatmeal cookie can still be on a plate."
    - Our version: "If a cookie is left in the jar, it is oatmeal."
  - **Keys, replaces Drawer R:** "Every key on the red ring opens a classroom."
    - Our version: "The rule says every key on the red ring opens a classroom. It does not say every key that opens a classroom is on the red ring."
- **Writing step:** the prompt is "Write your diagram as an if/then sentence.", the text box label is "Your if/then sentence", the button is "Compare my sentence", and the reference answer is labelled "Our version:".
- **Check your contrapositive:** the "Write it in English" step is skipped for items 1, 7, 8 and 9, where the sentence is already in if/then form.

**3. Mixed practice** (`OpeningMixedDrill.tsx`, `MixedFamilyPractice.tsx`, `mixedDrillItems.ts`, `opening-mixed-drill.css`)
- **Diagram stage:**
  - A reversed arrow shows only "Check which condition is enough to prove the other."
  - Repeated or extra words show "Use each condition once, one on each side. Extra or repeated words change the diagram. Check which condition is enough to prove the other."
  - A correct diagram shows "Right. Read it back: [readback]", followed by the rest of the explanation.
  - The shared diagram engine always prints the answer diagram. I couldn't edit that shared feature, so the drill hides that line with CSS after a wrong check.
- **Full condition labels**, mapped inside this drill only:
  - Mia trains → Mia qualifies
  - Person is a dancer → Person stretches
  - Student is admitted → Student is a senior
  - Person votes → Person is a member
  - Bridge opens → Bridge has been inspected
  - There is funding → There is approval
  - Person is a hiker → Person brings a map
- **The hard-coded 24** is now `baselineQuestions.length`.
- **Write-your-own:**
  - A wrong check shows only the partial, other or reversed feedback. The readback, the explanation and the "points to" line appear only after a correct check.
  - Question 6 prompt: "Which stalls does the rule allow to sell food at the festival?" "Under this statement" is now "under this rule".
  - Partial and other hints rewritten to stop naming the answer:
    - lightning: "The forecast alone isn't something that can be true or false. Ask: if what? Keep the whole clause." / "Moving indoors is what the rule proves, not what proves it. Look at the clause right after if."
    - choir: "A parent alone drops what the parent has to do. Ask: only if what? Keep the whole clause." / "Joining the trip is the condition that carries the requirement. Look at the clause after only if to find what joining needs."
    - bus: "Bus alone would cover daytime buses and buses that never use the depot. Ask: every what? Keep every detail that limits which buses the rule covers." / "Stopping at the hospital is what the rule proves, not the group it covers. Ask: every what?"
    - terrace: "A table drops a word that limits the requirement. Would any table do under this rule? Reread the words before required." / "Eating on the terrace is what needs something, so it is not the requirement. Required refers back to the idea that comes before it."
    - phone: "Phones alone would make a claim about every phone in the shop. Ask: the only what? Keep every word that limits the group." / "Arriving with a cracked screen is what every phone in the group has in common, not the group itself. Ask: the only what?"
    - stall: "Stalls alone loses the restriction. Ask: only what? Keep every word that limits which stalls." / "Selling food is what the restriction limits, not the stalls it allows. Ask: only what? The answer names which stalls sell food under this rule."
- **Choice items** (`mixedDrillItems.ts`):
  - condition-1, "Every swimmer": "Every swimmer includes people training later in the day. Reread the sentence for the words that limit which swimmers the rule covers."
  - condition-1, "Anyone who receives a wristband": "The wristband is what the rule proves, not the group it covers. Ask: every what? Keep every word that limits that group."
  - condition-2, "A locker key" (also fixes the grammar): "Locker key alone leaves out part of the description. Would any locker key open the cabinet under this rule? Keep every word that limits which keys qualify."
  - condition-2, "A key that opens the cabinet": "Opening the cabinet is what needs the key, so it is not the requirement. Ask: only what? The words right after only describe the key that is required."
  - condition-3, "A safety certificate": "Safety certificate alone allows an expired certificate. Which word in the sentence rules that out? Keep it in the label."
  - condition-3, "Leading the climbing group": "Leading the group is what needs something, so it is not the requirement. Ask: what is required? Look at the words before required."
  - transfer-1, option A: "The sentence has not said the timer is the only way to start the heater. A running heater could have been switched on another way. Ask: if what? Start your arrow from that condition."
  - transfer-1, option C is now "Timer is on → Heater runs", a qualifier error: "Timer is on drops part of the condition. The rule does not say the heater runs whenever the timer is on; it names a point the timer has to reach. Keep that detail in the condition."
  - transfer-2, "Trainee": "Trainee alone extends the rule to trainees without night-work certification. Keep every word that limits which trainees the rule covers."
  - confirm-direction, option A: "Printing is what the rule proves, not what proves something. The rule has not said every printed invitation means the registration was accepted. Ask: when what? Start from that condition."
  - confirm-direction, option C is now "Registration submitted → Invitation printed": "Submitted is not the same as accepted. A registration can be submitted and then turned down, and the rule promises nothing for it. Keep the exact condition the sentence uses."
  - confirm-qualification, "Courier": "Courier alone includes couriers carrying other deliveries. Keep every detail that limits which couriers the rule covers."
  - confirm-qualification, "Courier delivering medicine": "Delivering medicine includes medicine that is not refrigerated. That would broaden the group beyond the sentence. Which word did you drop?"
  - confirm-equivalence, option A: "Check where each sentence starts. Ask what only restricts in the first sentence, then compare it with the group every covers in the second."

**4. Lessons** (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx`)
- **"Find the complete condition" now teaches the Group 1 words.** It shows the Group 1 indicator lesson: the seven tabs (if, when, where, all, the only, every, any), the extra examples that cover whenever, each and people who, and the recall cards.
- **The complete-condition note is cut to one section.** The indicator lesson already teaches its registered-guest, report and late-applicant examples, so those three sections were dropped. What remains is "Keep the whole phrase before you place it": the "only people admitted" example, the notebook question, and the line "In the next drill, find the indicator, select the complete condition, place it beside the arrow and check that your diagram keeps the sentence's meaning."
- **"Only after" is added to the necessary-words lesson**, under the heading "Only after also marks a requirement":
  - Example: "The pharmacy fills a prescription only after a doctor signs it."
  - "Use the What Test: ask what the indicator refers to. Only after what? A doctor signs it. That signature is required before the pharmacy fills the prescription, so it goes right."
  - Diagram: Pharmacy fills the prescription → A doctor has signed it.
  - "Read it as: if the pharmacy fills a prescription, a doctor has signed it. The word after tells you the order of events in the sentence. The arrow still reads as must, not as a timeline."
  - Check question: "A doctor has signed Ana's prescription. Does this rule prove the pharmacy fills it?" Options: "Yes. The signature is enough." / "No. Whether it gets filled is still open."
  - Feedback: "The signature is what filling requires. Could a doctor sign a prescription that Ana never brings to the pharmacy? The rule would allow that." / "The signature is required, not enough. The rule tells you what every filled prescription has. It does not say every signed one gets filled."

## assistant

The drill fixes are finished and pushed to [PR #204](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/204), and I sent you the notification. All 318 bootcamp tests pass, and the type check and build both pass. I checked Spot the condition's entry page and Drill 2's hints in the browser. The other changed drills are covered by tests, but I haven't looked at them in the browser.

**Fixed:**
- **Wrong answers get a hint, never the answer,** in every drill. In Drills 2 to 7 a "Show me" button appears after a second miss on the same step, so nobody gets stuck. The student still has to enter the answer themselves. In the browser, choosing "valid" as an indicator got "“valid” is not an indicator in this sentence.", and "Show me" appeared on the second try.
- **Contrapositive drills now check the diagram and the contrapositive** as the student goes. Before, nothing was checked until the end of each sentence.
- **Untaught words are now taught.** The Group 1 word lesson (when, whenever, where, all, every, each, any, the only, people who) is now part of "Find the complete condition". "Only after" has its own section in the necessary-condition lesson, with a check question.
- **Grading is consistent.** "who", "Members who", "Only" or "Only those", and "Each and every" are now accepted wherever they make sense. The internal source notes are no longer shown to students.
- **Drill 3 is folded into Drill 2.** Its 13 sentences are now part of Drill 2, and the later drills are renumbered: Drills 3 to 7, Revisit Drills 4 and 5.
- **Spot the condition:** the rule-vs-fact questions come first, the "trigger" and "required outcome" wording is gone, and the entry page is titled "Spot the condition".
- **Build the sufficient side is down from 24 sentences to 13.** That's 10 new everyday sentences, one per Group 1 word, plus 3 practice items. The wrong choices now vary from item to item.
- **Item rewrites** for the problems the audit found:
  - Lightning: "you should expect thunder to follow"
  - Election: "the club election"
  - Errors: "must always be documented"
  - Four items in the later drills: three rewritten and one replaced (the Philadelphia sentence became "Ripe mangoes are always soft to the touch")
  - Mixed practice: about a dozen hints and choices

**Still open, for you:**
1. **The 51 contrapositives that start "If that…"** You didn't decide this one, so I left it alone.
2. **The lightning contrapositive reads stiffly:** "If it is not true that you should expect thunder to follow, then you do not see lightning."
3. **Spot the condition's entry page** still shows "01" on its decorative card, a leftover from "Drill 1". The drill pages also carry the label "INTRO TRAINING". Should either go?

The full audit, including the items that check out, is in [DRILL-AUDIT-2026-10-11.md](handoffs/codex-2026-10-10-conditional-bootcamp-thread/DRILL-AUDIT-2026-10-11.md).
